    89	#[test]
    90	fn pre_wave_closed_enums_reject_email_and_rounded_division() {
    91	    #[allow(dead_code)]
    92	    #[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
    93	    enum PreWaveTextFormat { Handle, Url }
    94	    #[allow(dead_code)]
    95	    #[derive(Debug, serde::Deserialize)]
    96	    enum PreWaveBuiltin { Count, Sum }
    97	
    98	    let email = serde_json::to_value(snapback4_core::schema::TextFormat::Email).unwrap();
    99	    let divide = serde_json::to_value(snapback4_core::ir::Builtin::Divide {
   100	        rounding: snapback4_core::ir::Rounding::HalfEven,
   101	        scale: Some(2),
   102	    }).unwrap();
   103	    assert!(serde_json::from_value::<PreWaveTextFormat>(email).unwrap_err().to_string().contains("unknown variant"));
   104	    assert!(serde_json::from_value::<PreWaveBuiltin>(divide).unwrap_err().to_string().contains("unknown variant"));
   105	}
     1	/-!
     2	# Wave 12 semantic witnesses
     3	
     4	These executable definitions cover language contracts outside the row IR:
     5	global count visibility, capture-time count provenance, email validation, and
     6	enum values entering the existing word-search path. Rounded division lives in
     7	`Quarry.Eval` because it is an IR builtin.
     8	-/
     9	
    10	namespace Quarry.Wave12
    11	
    12	def globalCount (rows : List α) : Nat := rows.length
    13	
    14	@[simp] theorem globalCount_empty : globalCount ([] : List α) = 0 := rfl
    15	
    16	theorem positive_count_has_member {rows : List α} (h : 0 < globalCount rows) :
    17	    ∃ row, row ∈ rows := by
    18	  cases rows with
    19	  | nil => simp [globalCount] at h
    20	  | cons row rows => exact ⟨row, by simp⟩
    21	
    22	/-- A readable absent global count is zero; hidden hit and miss are both
    23	`none`, so absence never becomes a disclosure channel. -/
    24	def visibleGlobalCount (authorized : Bool) (stored : Option Nat) : Option Nat :=
    25	  if authorized then some (stored.getD 0) else none
    26	
    27	example : visibleGlobalCount true none = some 0 := rfl
    28	example : visibleGlobalCount false none = none := rfl
    29	example : visibleGlobalCount false (some 7) = none := rfl
    30	
    31	/-- Binder provenance is a capture-time domain, transaction and authority
    32	triple. A retained diagnostic origin is intentionally not part of it. -/
    33	structure CountCapture where
    34	  domain : String
    35	  transaction : Nat
    36	  authority : String
    37	  definition : Nat
    38	  deriving DecidableEq
    39	
    40	def countCaptureLive (capture : CountCapture) (domain : String)
    41	    (transaction : Nat) (authority : String) (definition : Nat) : Bool :=
    42	  capture.domain == domain && capture.transaction == transaction &&
    43	    capture.authority == authority && capture.definition == definition
    44	
    45	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    46	    "entries/owner=alice" 4 "alice" 7 = true := rfl
    47	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    48	    "entries/owner=alice" 5 "alice" 7 = false := rfl
    49	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    50	    "entries/owner=bob" 4 "alice" 7 = false := rfl
    51	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    52	    "entries/owner=alice" 4 "bob" 7 = false := rfl
    53	example : countCaptureLive ⟨"entries/owner=alice", 4, "alice", 7⟩
    54	    "entries/owner=alice" 4 "alice" 8 = false := rfl
    55	
    56	/-- A keyed maintained count becomes required only for the exact key whose
    57	role predicate was proved in the same transaction. A readable miss is zero;
    58	hidden hits and misses share `none`. -/
    59	def keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard (guardKey readKey : String)
    60	    (sameTransaction authorized : Bool) (stored : Option Nat) : Option Nat :=
    61	  if sameTransaction && authorized && guardKey == readKey
    62	    then some (stored.getD 0)
    63	    else none
    64	
    65	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-a" true true none = some 0 := rfl
    66	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-b" true true (some 7) = none := rfl
    67	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-a" false true (some 7) = none := rfl
    68	example : keyedTotalAfterRoleGuard "dept-a" "dept-a" true false (some 7) = none := rfl
    69	
    70	def asciiAlphaNum (c : Char) : Bool :=
    71	  let n := c.toNat
    72	  (65 ≤ n && n ≤ 90) || (97 ≤ n && n ≤ 122) || (48 ≤ n && n ≤ 57)
    73	
    74	def asciiLower (c : Char) : Char :=
    75	  if 65 ≤ c.toNat && c.toNat ≤ 90 then Char.ofNat (c.toNat + 32) else c
    76	
    77	def splitOnce (needle : Char) : List Char → Option (List Char × List Char)
    78	  | [] => none
    79	  | c :: cs =>
    80	      if c == needle then some ([], cs) else
    81	        (splitOnce needle cs).map fun pair => (c :: pair.1, pair.2)
    82	
    83	def splitDots : List Char → List (List Char)
    84	  | [] => [[]]
    85	  | c :: cs =>
    86	      let rest := splitDots cs
    87	      if c == '.' then [] :: rest else
    88	        match rest with
    89	        | [] => [[c]]
    90	        | label :: labels => (c :: label) :: labels
    91	
    92	def noAdjacentDots : List Char → Bool
    93	  | '.' :: '.' :: _ => false
    94	  | _ :: cs => noAdjacentDots cs
    95	  | [] => true
    96	
    97	def localPunctuation : List Char :=
    98	  ['.', '!', '#', '$', '%', '&', '\'', '*', '+', '/', '=', '?', '^', '_', '`', '{', '|', '}', '~', '-']
    99	
   100	def localPartValid (part : List Char) : Bool :=
   101	  !part.isEmpty && part.length ≤ 64 && part.head? != some '.' &&
   102	    part.getLast? != some '.' && noAdjacentDots part &&
   103	    part.all fun c => asciiAlphaNum c || localPunctuation.contains c
   104	
   105	def domainLabelValid (label : List Char) : Bool :=
   106	  !label.isEmpty && label.length ≤ 63 &&
   107	    label.head?.any asciiAlphaNum && label.getLast?.any asciiAlphaNum &&
   108	    label.all fun c => asciiAlphaNum c || c == '-'
   109	
   110	def emailValid (email : String) : Bool :=
   111	  let chars := email.toList
   112	  chars.length ≤ 254 && chars.all (·.toNat ≤ 127) &&
   113	    chars.count '@' == 1 && match splitOnce '@' chars with
   114	      | some (localChars, domain) => localPartValid localChars &&
   115	          !domain.isEmpty && domain.length ≤ 253 && (splitDots domain).all domainLabelValid
   116	      | none => false
   117	
   118	example : emailValid "first.last+tag@sub.example" = true := by decide
   119	example : emailValid "x@localhost" = true := by decide
   120	example : emailValid "a..b@example.com" = false := by decide
   121	example : emailValid "a@-example.com" = false := by decide
   122	example : emailValid "a@пример.рф" = false := by decide
   123	
   124	/-- ASCII executable witness for enum spellings using the shared word-search
   125	shape: lowercase, alphanumeric token boundaries, and query-prefix matching.
   126	The Rust/device tests own NFC and the production Unicode tables. -/
   127	def asciiTokens (text : String) : List (List Char) :=
   128	  let rec go (remaining current : List Char) (tokens : List (List Char)) :=
   129	    match remaining with
   130	    | [] => (if current.isEmpty then tokens else current.reverse :: tokens).reverse
   131	    | c :: cs =>
   132	        if asciiAlphaNum c then go cs (asciiLower c :: current) tokens
   133	        else go cs [] (if current.isEmpty then tokens else current.reverse :: tokens)
   134	  go text.toList [] []
   135	
   136	def isPrefix : List Char → List Char → Bool
   137	  | [], _ => true
   138	  | _, [] => false
   139	  | p :: ps, w :: ws => p == w && isPrefix ps ws
   140	
   141	def enumMatchesAscii (value query : String) : Bool :=
   142	  let words := asciiTokens value
   143	  (asciiTokens query).all fun term => words.any fun word => isPrefix term word
   144	
   145	example : enumMatchesAscii "coffee-shop" "cof" = true := by decide
   146	example : enumMatchesAscii "book_store" "BOOK" = true := by decide
   147	example : enumMatchesAscii "museum" "music" = false := by decide
   148	
   149	end Quarry.Wave12

    82	    /// Resolve count provenance before rebinding `name`: a count domain may
    83	    /// itself refer to the old value of that local. Such a self-dependent
    84	    /// capture cannot be represented by the post-assignment binding and is
    85	    /// therefore deliberately not narrowed.
    86	    pub(super) fn count_capture(&self, name: &str, value: &A) -> Option<(String, BTreeSet<String>)> {
    87	        let A::Call { name: call, args, .. } = value else { return None };
    88	        if call != "count" || args.len() != 1 || self.catalog.functions.contains_key(call)
    89	            || !self.aggregate_guard_safe(value) { return None; }
    90	        let read = args.first().and_then(|arg| self.aggregate_domain(arg))?;
    91	        let (aggregate, roots) = self.aggregate_key(&read)?;
    92	        if roots.contains(name) { return None; }
    93	        Some((aggregate[1].clone(), roots))
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /// Attach pre-resolved capture-time provenance after assigning `name`.
    97	    /// `origins` remains diagnostic history; this field fact is the reaching
    98	    /// definition proof used by a later `require name > 0`.
    99	    pub(super) fn record_count_capture(&mut self, name: &str, capture: Option<(String, BTreeSet<String>)>) {
   100	        let Some((aggregate, mut owners)) = capture else { return };
   101	        let Some(result) = self.binding(name) else { return };
   102	        // The reaching definition is part of the marker. A later count over
   103	        // the same textual domain may restore a root marker after rebinding,
   104	        // but it must never make an older captured result current again.
   105	        let capture = vec![COUNT_CAPTURE.into(), aggregate,
   106	            format!("{}:{}", result.runtime, result.revision)];
   107	        owners.insert(name.to_owned());
   108	        for owner in owners {
   109	            let Some(mut binding) = self.binding(&owner).cloned() else { return };
   110	            binding.fields.insert(capture.clone(), Ty::Bool);
   111	            self.scopes.last_mut().expect("a scope").insert(owner, binding);
   112	        }
   113	    }
   114	
   115	    fn count_key(&self, value: &A) -> Option<(Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>)> {
   116	        let (count_arg, captured_name) = match value {
   117	            A::Call { name, args, .. } if name == "count" && args.len() == 1
   118	                && !self.catalog.functions.contains_key(name) => args.first(),
   119	            A::Name { name, .. } => self.binding(name)?.origins.iter().rev().find_map(|(_, origin)| match origin {
   120	                A::Call { name, args, .. } if name == "count" && args.len() == 1
   121	                    && !self.catalog.functions.contains_key(name) => args.first(),
   122	                _ => None,
   123	            }),
   124	            _ => None,
   125	        }.map(|arg| (arg, match value { A::Name { name, .. } => Some(name.as_str()), _ => None }))?;

   136	            }
   137	        }

   141	    fn aggregate_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<(Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>)> {
   142	        if read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() { return None; }
   143	        let mut roots = BTreeSet::new();
   144	        let keys = read.keys.iter().map(|key| Some(format!("{:?}:{}", key.column,
   145	            self.aggregate_value(&key.value, &mut roots)?))).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()?;
   146	        let seeks = read.seeks.iter().map(|seek| Some(format!("{}:{}:{}", seek.column, seek.op,
   147	            self.aggregate_value(&seek.value, &mut roots)?))).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>()?;
   148	        let filter = match read.filter.as_deref() {
   149	            Some(value) => Some(self.aggregate_value(value, &mut roots)?),
   150	            None => None,
   151	        };
   152	        if roots.is_empty() { roots.insert(pure_calls::CONSTANTS.into()); }
   153	        let identity = format!("{:?}:{:?}:{:?}:{keys:?}:{seeks:?}:{filter:?}", read.table, read.index, read.order_keys);
   154	        Some((vec![NONEMPTY.into(), identity], roots))
   155	    }
   156	
   157	    fn aggregate_value(&self, value: &A, roots: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> Option<String> {

codex
I found a provenance gap: a stale count can pick up a fresh capture marker when its local is also a dependency of the fresh count. The guard lookup checks the domain but does not verify that the marker belongs to the guarded result’s current definition. I’m tracing a concrete counterexample and finishing the remaining audit.

+/// Exact rounded division comes from its own stream, preserving every older
+/// case whose stream does not select the new expression.
+fn rounded_division(case: &mut Case) {
+    let mut rng = Rng::new(case.seed ^ ROUND_DIV_STREAM);
+    if rng.pick(6) != 0 || !matches!(case.program.body, Body::Scalar(_)) { return; }
+    let modes = [Rounding::TowardZero, Rounding::AwayFromZero, Rounding::Floor,
+        Rounding::Ceiling, Rounding::HalfAwayFromZero, Rounding::HalfEven];
+    let dividend = if rng.pick(2) == 0 { Expr::Arg("a".into(), Type::Int) }
+        else { Expr::Int([-7, -5, -1, 0, 1, 5, 7][rng.pick(7) as usize]) };
+    let divisor = if rng.pick(2) == 0 { Expr::Arg("b".into(), Type::Int) }
+        else { Expr::Int([-3, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3][rng.pick(7) as usize]) };
+    let scale = match rng.pick(5) { 0 => None, 1 => Some(0), 2 => Some(2), 3 => Some(6), _ => Some(12) };
+    case.program.body = Body::Scalar(Expr::RoundedDivide(
+        modes[rng.pick(modes.len() as u64) as usize].clone(), scale, Box::new(dividend), Box::new(divisor)));
+}
+
 /// Add lower-bound rows from an independent stream. Every selected case has
 /// null-open, before-boundary, exact-boundary and future rows.
 fn timed_disclosure(case: &mut Case) {

+/-- Truncating quotient/remainder followed by one exact rounding step. The
+model deliberately derives truncation from the existing Euclidean primitive,
+so its oracle is independent of the Rust implementation. -/
+def roundedDiv (rounding : Rounding) (scale : Option Nat) (x y : Int) : EM Value := do
+  if y = 0 then refuse "E_DIV_ZERO"
+  let x := x * (10 : Int) ^ scale.getD 0
+  let qe := Int.ediv x y
+  let re := Int.emod x y
+  let q := if x < 0 ∧ re ≠ 0 then qe + (if y < 0 then -1 else 1) else qe
+  let r := x - q * y
+  if r = 0 then checkI53 q else
+    let direction : Int := if (x < 0) = (y < 0) then 1 else -1
+    let away := q + direction
+    let result := match rounding with
+      | .towardZero => q
+      | .awayFromZero => away
+      | .floor => if direction < 0 then away else q
+      | .ceiling => if 0 < direction then away else q
+      | .halfAwayFromZero => if Int.natAbs r * 2 ≥ Int.natAbs y then away else q
+      | .halfEven =>
+          if Int.natAbs r * 2 > Int.natAbs y ∨
+              (Int.natAbs r * 2 = Int.natAbs y ∧ Int.emod q 2 ≠ 0)
+          then away else q
+    checkI53 result
+
 /-- A calendar builtin over unbounded integers; the caller checks i53. A
 non-positive period or an offset outside `[0, period)` is unreachable from a
 checked program. -/
@@ -930,6 +955,12 @@ def evalExpr : Nat → Ctx → View → Env → Option Value → Expr → EM (Va
         | some r => return (← checkI53 r, none)
         | none => typeErr
       | _, _ => typeErr
+    | .roundDiv rounding scale a b => do
+      let (x, _) ← ev a
+      let (y, _) ← ev b
+      match x, y with
+      | .s (.int x), .s (.int y) => return (← roundedDiv rounding scale x y, none)
+      | _, _ => typeErr
     | .arr xs => do return (.arr (← evs xs), none)
     | .record fs => do
       let vs ← fs.mapM fun p => do return (p.1, (← ev p.2).1)
# Wave 12 language-lane status

Updated: 2026-10-15

## Scope

This lane owns the five semantic gaps: ungrouped maintained totals, sound
aggregate and maintained-result narrowing, email text format, enum search,
and exact rounded decimal division. It does not own engine expiry,
`maintained_id`, parser recovery, reserved-name diagnostics or the branch
planner.

## Progress

- Governing LLPs, guide cards, wave evidence lanes 019/021/027/083/091/093,
  NA10 and the pattern ledger read.
- Baseline gate `/tmp/lang-baseline-ready` observed before production edits.
- LLP 3000.013.001 records the semantic and verification contract and is
  linked into `llp/current/`.
- Email schema/runtime/client support landed in `b081f96054`.
- The remaining semantic foundation landed in `0fe7cc1263`: ungrouped global
  maintained counts, enum search, exact three/four-argument rounded division,
  and capture-time count provenance with stale-proof invalidation.
- Cross-runtime evidence landed in `4156cdf7e5`: generated rounded-division
  cases cover every mode/scale and compile into the Lean fragment; one drawn
  representative executes through native, Lean and both device stores.
  Simulator and device scenarios exercise all five surfaces; standalone Lean witnesses
  cover the semantic fragments outside the row evaluator.
- The shared Lean lowering compile blocker reported in the coordination inbox
  is repaired; both Wave 12 language test executables compile again.
- Affected language tests pass 6/6. Simulator, difftest generation, memory
  device and SQLite device tests pass 1/1 each. Lean passes 54 build jobs, 65
  audited theorems, 88/88 examples and 7/7 fixtures. The focused CLI guide
  lookup regression passes, and `ref-check --verbose` passes.
- The full guide test currently passes 120/123. Its three residual failures
  are in concurrent, uncommitted diagnostics documentation: the maintained
  disclosure example names an undeclared table and exceeds its card budget,
  a personas block duplicates the project identity declaration, and their
  added prose moves the corpus-wide reduction ratio. Wave 12's modified guide
  examples, compiled-block preservation, language execution and email
  expectation all pass in that same run.
- The first blind pair found stale count recapture, global page
  misclassification and an email diagnostic-code mismatch; `108c2aeeab`
  closes those counterexamples. The second pair found that the global scalar
  still entered loop/aggregate domains and that one IR assertion was stale.
  The fixes now reject loop, aggregate and `exists` use, assert the actual
  point-read IR, and pass `maintains` 15/15.
- The third blind pair returned Muse READY and Codex NOT READY with one exact
  expression-extrema fallback that could recover a global view after ordinary
  aggregate-domain rejection. The intent registry and typed-binder fallback
  now both exclude ungrouped targets, with `min` and `max` regressions. Global
  scalar declarations also refuse `viewer`, `now`, `commit` and `new` rather
  than shadowing contextual builtins. Wave 12 semantics pass 7/7.
- Closed-enum compatibility evidence proves a pre-wave decoder rejects both
  `email` and `Divide` as unknown variants; the full device scenario and the
  compatibility witness pass 2/2. The coordinated focused generated family
  for all five semantics is pending its owner-provided result before the next
  same-revision review pair.

## Concerns / decisions

- Count non-emptiness is an exact transaction fact, never a general theorem
  prover; conservative misses are acceptable, stale acceptance is not.
- The intermediate census's owner-or-manager structural-fanout refusals are
  in the planner/diagnostics lanes: this lane does not change OR-role planning.
- Hidden maintained misses remain null. Zero is produced only after an
  authorization proof.
- Rounded division uses exact signed integer quotient/remainder and an
  explicit enum mode. The four-argument integer form also names result scale,
  so fractional averages need no manual fixed-point multiply; no floating
  point or implicit `avg` is introduced.

8448:### CUT fix review — 2026-10-12
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**

Keep 0.4.28. Extend both packed proof and starter-toolchain qualification to
every supported frontend/template pair, twice; require their complete matrices
in imported Linux evidence. Browser execution belongs to the Linux host; clean

Runtime history acknowledgment is independent of successful compiler snapshots.

implicit downgrade before compilation/install, with an explicit --to remedy.

a truly empty definition from zero standalone cells covered by a dormant row.
The widening note says rows that later use those values. LLP 3000.000.000 §11
and the deploy card document the disjoint counts. Validation/review follows.

### 3.2 Maintained views: aggregates

```quarry
view followerCount: count follows by .followedId
view receivedLikes: count likes by .postId.authorId
view dailyPosts: count posts where not .removed by .authorId, day(.at)
view revenue: sum orders of .amount by .shopId

table posts:
  likeCount: count likes
```

These have exactly the semantics of today's maintains: the same engine, the
same recorded attribution, and maintained columns for the in-table form.
`followerCount[p]` reads the number, 0 when absent. Two defaults remove the
boilerplate lines:

- **Readability is derived.** A uniformly readable source gives a readable
  aggregate. A source whose read rule uses only bare group keys gives the same
  rule over the aggregate's key. Anything else needs an explicit `read <-`.
- **In-table columns find their reference.** A column is counted by the one
  column of the source that references the table. If there are several, use
  `count likes by .postId`.

### 3.3 Materialized views: Following
### 5.1 Streaming aggregates

`count(R)`, `sum(R, .col)` and `exists R`, where `R` is a predicate read
without a page clause, examine rows under the examined and steps meters.
Each decoded row is charged transiently, and exactly that charge is released
when the row is dropped. Retained state stays charged.

A streaming consumer keeps the underlying scan's admission, authorization,
expiry and coverage checks. Calling a scan `count` does not make it a safe
probe; LLP 3000.007 §21.9's expiring-mutation counterexample stands.
Unpaged aggregate reads retain ordinary streaming scans: membership, enum
complements and window predicates do not become bounded merge pages. Their
residual filters fold the entire admitted domain, or return null on a cut.
Streaming aggregation across planned lanes is not implemented.

The busy-chat digest claim (LLP 3000.011.001.000's 16 MiB stop) holds only
if the retained recipient-heavy fixture passes.

An inferred aggregate seek must preserve predicate short-circuiting: its
endpoint must already be present and must not fail when evaluated before the
whole predicate. Otherwise the comparison stays a residual filter. Selecting
one safe endpoint must not extract an unsafe sibling bound on the same column.
A named prefix pinning intrinsic `id` keeps its single-row access path; remaining
comparisons filter that row rather than introducing an order after the id pin.

### 5.2 Self-chunking jobs
### 14.7 A hidden keyed maintained value is unavailable, including at zero

A keyed maintained point authorizes the requested group before disclosing
whether storage contains it. If admitted, a stored group returns its value and
an absent group returns the maintained operation's identity, currently zero
for `count` and `sum`. If the point's read rule hides the group, a stored group
and an absent group both return `null`; the absent case must never fall through
the authorized-miss coercion and become zero. A query may instead `require` the
same authority condition first and return its chosen refusal code.

The authorization decision is identical for stored and synthetic groups even
when the aggregate's source expires. The actual system principal bypasses the view
rule for a synthetic miss just as it does for a stored hit, and therefore sees
the identity. That policy bypass does not bypass a read-through parent's
lifetime: an inherited group remains nullable until an exact live-parent point
witness proves the parent exists. A row yielded by a fresh table read can carry
that witness through a loop only when the whole loop body is proven write-free;
saved or buffered row values do not renew it, and a writeful loop needs a
current point guard in each iteration.
Because an unguarded private point can return `null`, its static result is a
nullable number. A matching effect-safe authority proof may narrow that result
to the maintained operation's required numeric type. Maintained-rule focal
fields stay scoped to the maintained target row; only declared group fields
are replaced with caller key expressions, so an unrelated caller projection
cannot supply a target-only rule field. A
write, relevant rebinding, or entry into and exit from a viewer-scoped block
invalidates that proof; a job inside `as principal` has that principal's
authority rather than the system exemption. Loop headers retain only facts
that survive every iteration, including facts on an outer binding while a
loop item shadows its source name, separately from local assignment revisions.
Program `exists` observes only rows readable by its current viewer, while an
`exists` witness inside the maintained rule tests raw presence below the
target table's read rule. A positive program witness therefore proves raw
presence, but a negative one proves raw absence only for the actual system
scope, a uniformly readable target, or a complete point key whose selected row
is structurally readable. Matching text alone does not turn a hidden miss into
a raw-absence authority proof. Authored rule constants are resolved before
group-key expressions are substituted. A parameter, local or loop item named
`allow`, `public` or `deny` remains a lexical operand rather than acquiring the
rule constant's meaning.

`check` emits `N_MAINTAIN_AUTHORIZATION` when a query directly returns a bare,
fully keyed maintained point whose read rule is narrower than the set of query
callers and no dominating `require` proves the same authority condition for
the current key and authority scope.
An unrelated requirement does not silence it. The note names the three
observable states—value, authorized zero and hidden `null`—and recommends the
explicit `require`. A regression exercises both a stored and absent group for
an authorized caller, an unauthorized caller, and a second principal admitted
by an `exists` branch of the view rule.
### 5.1 The seven forms (rev 1)

| # | Today | Proposed | Source | Bound by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A local binds once ("duplicate local") | `x = expr` rebinds a local of the same type; a `for` may carry a counter or a running sum | S | the step meter; `Stmt::Assign` exists |
| 2 | No accumulators | `xs = []` then `xs = xs + [v]` (the binder emits `Push`); `s = s + n`; a list built in a loop is bounded by the heap meter and may be iterated | S | the heap meter (`Push` charges) and the step meter |
| 3 | `continue` refused | `continue` | S (minimalism) | `Stmt::Continue` exists |
| 4 | `else if` refused | `else if cond:` as sugar for the nesting (`elif` is a guess `check --fix` rewrites, §5.5) | S (minimalism) | nothing to bound |
| 5 | `where` refused | `t[prefix] [after c] first\|last N by idx where <predicate>`: the scan proceeds in index order; a row passing the read rule and the predicate counts toward N; examined rows and probes are metered | S + A's completeness class | the examined-rows and probe meters; §5.6 fixes completeness and cursors |
| 6 | Text `+` refused | `text + text` → `Concat`; `slice`, `tokens`, `count(text)`, `lower(text)`; no `format`, no interpolation | S (bytes) and a product discipline | the output and heap meters |
| 7 | `/` and `%` refused | `a / b` (integer quotient) and `a % b` beside `divmod`; zero refuses `E_DIV_ZERO` | S (a semantic preference) | nothing to bound |

**2026-09-27 — list expressions (0.4.5 lane QE).** `a + b` concatenates
two lists in every expression position, including returns, arguments and
record fields. Item types join as in list literals: incompatible nonempty
items refuse immediately, including references to different tables and
conflicts nested inside records or lists. Empty lists supply no competing
item type. Compatible scalar items retain a common representation. Two enums
join to their deduplicated variant union, including subsets and equivalent sets
in different orders; an existing superset keeps its order, otherwise the union
follows encounter order. Quarry imposes no variant-count limit. Self-append
checks both operands against the joined item type, so disjoint enums also
keep their original string values. Enum and text widen to text bounded by both
the longest variant and the text operand,
under the existing text character-domain rule. If an enum item cannot join,
the diagnostic teaches a typed identity helper such as
`fn state(v: enum('on', 'off')): v`, with all variants, and
`[state(a), state(b)]` when every value belongs to that enum; this preserves
values without conversion. Generic id with a reference or
principal becomes id; int with decimal or money retains that numeric type
without rescaling; json accepts other known, non-row values. Unknown is never
a widening candidate in a nonempty item join. A mixed-reference refusal teaches
the intentional loss of table specificity: `fn exposedId(value: id): value`,
then `[exposedId(x), exposedId(y)]`, or `exposedId(row.id)` in each projected
field for a nested `[].id` conflict. An effect's `once [recipient, period]` is a positional key tuple,
not an iterable list, so its coordinates may differ in type. Bounds add, saturating
at the existing meter-bound sentinel. The shared `Concat` builtin charges
the result allocation. `xs = xs + [x]` retains its in-place `Push` lowering.
Other loop-carried concatenations retain the existing heap-bound widening
when their item type stabilizes; recursive item growth refuses with a
self-append rewrite using a fixed item type. A list plus a non-list names
both operand types and suggests `xs + [x]`. The recursive-growth rewrite
also covers concatenations within conditional assignments and failures caught
at reassignment, after the assigned expression typechecks; an ill-typed `+`
keeps its operand diagnostic without a recursive-growth cascade. Consuming
the accumulator in a scalar, such as `xs = xs + [count(xs)]`, also keeps the
ordinary item-join diagnostic; only embedding the accumulator in the resulting
container earns recursive-growth guidance.
The length census prices bounded append and prepend increments
like `Push`, including the initial contents; doubling and unresolved cyclic or
open growth retain the step-ceiling fallback. No runtime ceiling changes.

### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes

**8. `fn` takes a body.** Today `fn` is one expression and `proc` is a
block that returns nothing, so the 0.1.0 builder wrote the receipt
fan-out three times (`send`, `sendMedia`, `sendPoll`) and the 0.2.6
builder wrote its receipt state as three nested one-line `fn`s. LLP
2000.000 §6 named the split as pricing residue. Proposed: one `fn`,
declared with an expression or with a block ending in `return`, callable
in expression position anywhere; expanded inline as helpers are today
(`bind/program/control.rs`), so a write inside a body is admitted exactly
when the caller may write. **Loop placement also inherits the caller's
enclosing loops:** a helper inside a job's recipient sweep performs ordinary
per-recipient loops; a helper at the job's top level still receives the
automatic-sweep and stable-domain checks. `break` and `continue` remain
lexically scoped to loops inside the helper. A query calling a writing `fn`
is a placement error at the call site, with the site named. `proc` is deleted with the
rewrite `fn` (rules: delete, don't deprecate).

```quarry
fn receiptState(cid: conversations, sender: principal, seq: int):
  unread = 0
  undelivered = 0
  for m in memberships[cid] first 50 by byConversationPrincipal:
    if m.principal = sender or m.joinedSequence >= seq:
      continue
    if m.readSequence < seq:
      unread = unread + 1
    if m.deliveredSequence < seq:
      undelivered = undelivered + 1
  return 'read' if unread = 0 else ('delivered' if undelivered = 0 else 'sent')
```

**2026-09-27 — repeated pure calls (0.4.5 lane QE, calibration #22).**
A null test narrows a repeated `fn` call like a field path of a lexical
snapshot when the expanded helper reads no table, writes nothing and reads
neither `now` nor another changing input (the commit/read clock, a minted id
or connection state). Every argument must be a lexical name, a field path
of a lexical snapshot, a literal, or another such pure call. For example,
`now + windows.ttl(c.disappearing) if windows.ttl(c.disappearing) != null else null`
compiles. A database read used directly as an argument does not qualify;
binding its result once creates a lexical snapshot.

The null fact identifies the helper and ordered arguments, including each
snapshot's lexical identity. It is scoped to the guarded branch, short circuit
or surviving guard and invalidated when any argument root is reassigned or
carried through a changing loop. The binder checks the expanded IR, including
transitive helpers and named shapes, for effects; it never removes a read,
write or clock access to make a call pure. Both calls still execute in source
order under the existing meters. Every other optional call keeps the existing
located `E_QUARRY_TYPE` diagnostic and “bind the optional call once” rewrite.

**2026-10-03 — guarded point reads (lane QE2, calibration #30).**
`require exists t[k]`, `require t[k] != null`, and the present branch of
`if t[k] != null` prove the identical unique-key lookup non-null, including
helper arguments. The binder records stable key identities in the current
lexical state; rebindings, writes (including expanded expression helpers),
branch joins, loop fixed points and job viewer changes invalidate stale facts. Guards containing
writing helpers establish no point fact. Both reads still execute and are
metered. This does not make a table-reading helper pure.

A required reference is a typed id, not an existence constraint. Even when a
parent's read rule is exactly the membership probe just required, a membership
can outlive an absent parent. No parent-existence implication is inferred.
The nullable helper-argument diagnostic names `require exists
conversations[conversation] else NOT_FOUND` or a bind with `! else NOT_FOUND`.
Enum-to-text widening already admits every variant whose character length
fits the destination bounds; nullable enums still need their own null guard.

**9. A job may have a prelude.** Today a job's body is `chunk` sweeps and
nothing else (`parser/programs.rs:73`); both 0.2.6 builders hit it
("jobs must start with chunk"; "job-level binding outside a chunk").
Proposed: statements before the first `chunk` form a prelude that runs at
the start of every chunk under that chunk's meter — argument-derived
bindings, a config row, `divmod` of the period — with its bindings
visible in every chunk. Re-running it per chunk keeps a chunk wholly
done or not started; a prelude that writes is refused (a write belongs
to a chunk).

### 5.3 Data access: seeks, groups, a page is a list
### 5.6 `where`: completeness, the cursor, and reads inside it

A filtered page is `complete: true` when it holds N matches or the scan
reached the end of its range, and `complete: false` when the meter cut
it. `Scan` gains one field, `filter: Option<Expr>`; the runner applies it
inside the scan loop after the read rule; `pages.rs`'s page-plan analysis
and `N_NO_CURSOR` treat a filtered scan as a scan; on a device the same
code runs and `beyond_horizon` is unchanged.

**The cursor never positions after a hidden row.** Rev 1 said a cut page
continues after the last *examined* row, so a continuation could not
re-examine the rejected run; the received review pointed out that a
cursor is base64url over the index key values (`cursor.rs:51`) and that
the last examined row may be one the read rule hides, whose key the
viewer must not learn (LLP 3000 §7, "completeness is a class": the cut
site stays off the wire; a hidden row costs what a miss costs). The rule
is therefore: a filled page's `next` is after its last returned row; a
cut page's `next` is after the last **rule-visible** examined row —
returned or filter-rejected — and never after a hidden one. A visible
row's key is the viewer's to see whether or not the author's predicate
kept it, so nothing is disclosed, and a continuation still advances past
every filter-rejected run. On a uniformly authorized range (the rule
decided by the prefix) every examined row is visible and the two rules
coincide. On a non-uniform range the page keeps its existing class —
never `complete` (`N_NEVER_COMPLETE`), and a cut inside a hidden run ends
on an empty capped page as it does today — so `where` adds no channel
the non-uniform class did not already close.

**2026-09-17 — returned-shape refinement (W13h, descoped in R5).**
The source page is materialized before its returned rows are shaped.
The rule applies only to a query's top-level returned shape expression,
`return <base> { ... }`: the base is an inline scan or merge, or a variable
with exactly one definition, that definition a read, no reassignment and no
other use anywhere in the program. The binder marks that lowered loop; only
that loop drops a row whose shape the meter starved and every later row.
`next` reaches the unfinished row first. A preceding fully computed row
supplies its exclusive position; otherwise the first unfinished rule-visible
row supplies an inclusive position — provided the page's own materialization was
not cut: a drain-cut merge with no fully computed emission, an opening cut or a
word-limit cut retains no continuation. A shaped collector that is not the returned
expression keeps today's behavior: its empty or partial shape stays on the page,
with the existing continuation ownership and position, without trimming or rewind.
This includes assigned shapes, records, conditionals, helpers, aliases and
collector payloads; some such compositions have no continuation owner.
The answer stays `complete: false` on a meter cut, with `cut: 'meter'` on
devices and uniform server reads; non-uniform server reads keep `cut: null`.
The scan frontier and a cut inside the predicate retain the rule above.
A cut on a nested page still makes the answer incomplete; it does not
discharge any cut in `usePage`.

Reads inside the predicate (`where exists likes[.id, viewer]`) are
proposed as admitted: each is a rule probe the probe meter already
counts, and `check --cost` prints the class as "probe × examined". Rev 1
deferred this; the Trending and grouped-search shapes want it, and the
meter bounds it.

### 5.7 What stays refused, and why it is not S
### 6.6 The audience is the read rule

Today a synced table spells its audience twice: `read <- exists
memberships[.conversationId, viewer]` and `sync to
memberships[.conversationId]`, and `check` refuses any mismatch with a
rewrite (`E_SYNC`, `bind/schema.rs:51`). LLP 3000 §7 already binds
delivery to the rule's probe, so the second line adds no information the
engine does not derive. Muse spent six compile iterations here; Grok
named it its main friction. Proposed: `sync` with no `to` clause, with
or without a horizon, means *the audience my read rule states*:

```quarry
table messages:
  ...
  read <- exists memberships[.conversationId, viewer]
  sync last 500 by byConvTime           -- to memberships[.conversationId], from the rule

table todos:
  ...
  read <- .ownerId = viewer
  sync                                  -- to .ownerId

table notices:
  ...
  public 'notices are public'
  sync last 100 by byTime               -- public
```

The three invertible rule forms infer to the three sync forms; a target
with several principal columns still needs `by .col`, and `check` says
so; a rule that is not one of the three refuses `E_SYNC` with the rewrite
as today. `sync to …` remains valid and, when written, must equal what
the rule implies — the check that exists becomes a consistency check on
an optional clause rather than a requirement to restate.

### 6.7 A maintain that reaches every device says so

A `maintain` grouped by a public source's audience columns inherits
`sync public`: one value row per group on every device. The 0.2.6 Astra
lane's repost counter did exactly that — one row per post replicated to
every device, a cold-start timeout at the 50% checkpoint, and a
schema change mid-replay. Proposed: `N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED` names every
maintain whose inherited audience is public or a wide group, with the
row count it implies on the fixture when one is loaded, and `online
only` on the maintain opts it out of inheritance; `check --residency`
lists it (§6.3).

    38	    pub(super) fn prefix_bound(&self, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, position: Position) -> Fallible<(ast::RangeSpec, Bound)> {

    40	        let ceiling = if self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() }.examined;
    41	        let pinned = &index.columns[..read.keys.len()];
    42	        // One group per distinct source key: a parent-scoped source cap bounds
    43	        // its aggregate rows too, without copying a constraint onto the view.
    44	        // A pinned unique key bounds the group as a cap does (wave 3 #7); `.id = x`
    45	        // plans as the closed seek `.id >= x and .id <= x` on the next column.
    46	        let mut equal = pinned.to_vec();
    47	        if let Some(next) = index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) {
    48	            let printed = |op: &str| read.seeks.iter().filter(|seek| &seek.column == next && seek.op == op).map(|seek| crate::print::expression(&seek.value)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    49	            let (low, high) = (printed(">="), printed("<="));
    50	            if read.seeks.len() == 2 && low.len() == 1 && low == high { equal.push(next.clone()); }
    51	        }
    52	        let cap = self.schema.group_bound(&read.table, pinned).into_iter().chain(crate::bind::unique_bound::unique_bound(self.schema, &read.table, &equal)).min().or_else(|| {
    53	            let m = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table)?;
    54	            let parent = m.parent_key.as_ref()?;
    55	            pinned.contains(&parent.reference).then(|| self.schema.group_bound(&m.source, &[parent.reference.clone()])).flatten()
    56	        });
    57	        let bound = if position == Position::Exists { Bound::Literal(1) }
    58	        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.22 — ruling 24: the schema cap supplies the whole group's ordinary literal scan bound.
    59	        // Queries with a non-uniform rule keep this existing capped path;
    60	        // scan_at upgrades uniformly decided aggregates to streaming below.
    61	        else if position == Position::Aggregate && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query && let Some(cap) = cap.filter(|cap| u64::from(*cap) <= ceiling) { Bound::Literal(cap) }
    62	        else if position == Position::Aggregate || (position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job) { Bound::Meter }
    63	        else if let Some(cap) = cap.filter(|cap| u64::from(*cap) <= ceiling) { Bound::Literal(cap) }
    64	        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.22 — ruling 23: only query loop and lane domains may consume a prefix under the examined meter.
    65	        else if position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query { Bound::Meter }
    66	        else {
    67	            let columns = if pinned.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!(" by {}", pinned.join(", ")) };
    68	            let mut page = read.clone();
    69	            page.index = Some(index.name.clone());
    70	            // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.3 — a synced table's horizon keeps the newest rows, so its page reads newest first:
    71	            // the page rewrite and N_NO_HORIZON's window then agree, and applying both draws no N_SYNC_ORDER.
    72	            let window = self.schema.table(&read.table).and_then(|table| table.sync.as_ref()).map(|sync| sync.horizon.as_ref().map(|window| window.by.clone()));
    73	            if let Some(Some(by)) = &window
    74	                && let Some(horizon) = self.catalog.tables.get(&read.table).and_then(|info| info.indexes.iter().find(|candidate| &candidate.name == by))
    75	                && horizon.words.is_none() && horizon.columns.len() > pinned.len() && horizon.columns[..pinned.len()] == *pinned {
    76	                page.index = Some(horizon.name.clone());
    77	            }
    78	            let order = if window.is_some() { ast::Order::Desc } else { ast::Order::Asc };
    79	            page.range = Some(ast::RangeSpec { order, take: 50, cursor: Some(Box::new(A::Name { name: "c".into(), site: read.site.clone() })), site: read.site.clone() });
    80	            let suggestion = format!("page with `{} N` and a cursor: `{}` (declare `c: cursor ?`)", if window.is_some() { "last" } else { "first" }, self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(page.clone())));
    81	            // A filter pinning a whole unique key names at most one row: offer the one-row read first.
    82	            let one = (index.unique && index.words.is_none() && !pinned.is_empty() && pinned.len() == index.columns.len()).then(|| {
    83	                let mut one = page.clone();
    84	                one.range = Some(ast::RangeSpec { order: ast::Order::Asc, take: 1, cursor: None, site: read.site.clone() });
    85	                format!("`unique {}` makes this at most one row: `{}`; or ", pinned.join(", "), self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(one)))
    86	            }).unwrap_or_default();
    87	            let rewrite = if cap.is_none() { format!("{one}declare `cap N{columns}` on {}, or {suggestion}", read.table) } else { suggestion };
    88	            let message = match cap {
    89	                Some(cap) => format!("{}'s whole group has cap {cap}, above the {ceiling} examined ceiling; one read cannot examine the whole group", read.table),
    90	                None => format!("{} has no cap over this prefix; this position needs a bounded page", read.table),
    91	            };
    92	            return Err(if position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Query {
    93	                placement(&read.site, format!("{message}; meter-bounded prefix loops and lane sources belong in queries"), rewrite).guide("bounds")

    99	        Ok((ast::RangeSpec { order: ast::Order::Asc, take: bound.maximum() as u32, cursor: None, site: read.site.clone() }, bound))

#[test]
fn fresh_trending_has_no_writable_score_and_an_explicit_public_device_variant() {
    let source = snapback4_lang::guide::quarry(include_str!(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/../snapback4-lang/tests/fixtures/legacy-feed-recipes.md"))).into_iter()
        .find(|s| s.contains("query rollingLikes():")).unwrap();
    let private = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.clone())]);
    assert!(private.diagnostics.is_empty() && private.notes.is_empty());
    assert!(!private.schema.tables.contains_key("ranked"));
    assert!(private.schema.schedules.is_empty());
    for name in ["rollingLikes"] { assert_eq!(private.schema.operations[name].runs.as_deref(), Some("server")); }
    let forged = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("{source}\nmutation forge(postId: posts):\n  update likeCounts[postId] {{ value: 999 }}\n"))]);
    assert!(forged.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.message == "likeCounts is a maintained aggregate; write its source rows instead"), "{:?}", forged.diagnostics);
    let horizon = private.schema.tables["posts"].sync.as_ref().unwrap().horizon.as_ref().unwrap();
    assert_eq!((horizon.last, horizon.by.as_str()), (2000, "byTime"));
    let public = source.replace("  read <- .principal = viewer", "  read <- allow")
        .replace("  sync to .principal", "  sync public").replace("  online only\n", "")
        .replace("  sync public last 2000 by byTime", "  sync public");
    let public = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), public)]);
    assert!(public.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", public.diagnostics);
    assert_eq!(public.notes.iter().map(|d| d.code.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED"]);
    assert!(public.schema.tables["posts"].sync.as_ref().unwrap().horizon.is_none());
    assert!(public.schema.tables["likes"].sync.as_ref().unwrap().horizon.is_none());
    assert!(public.schema.tables["likeCounts"].sync.is_some());
}

#[test]
fn w14a_hour_lanes_are_the_first_trending_answer() {
    let feeds = include_str!(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/../snapback4-lang/tests/fixtures/legacy-feed-recipes.md"));
    let first = feeds.split("## Trending:").nth(1).unwrap();
    assert!(first.starts_with(" posts younger than a day, ranked in the like's own transaction"));
    let source = snapback4_lang::guide::quarry(first).into_iter().next().unwrap();
    assert!(source.contains("likeCount: count likes by postId"));
    assert!(source.contains("insert <- .principal = viewer"));
    assert!(source.contains("immutable postId, principal"));
    assert!(source.contains("unique byPair: postId, principal"));
    assert!(source.contains("delete <- .principal = viewer"));
    assert_eq!(source.lines().filter(|line| line.starts_with("mutation ")).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        ["mutation create(body: text <=500):", "mutation setLike(postId: posts, liked: bool):", "mutation remove(postId: posts):"]);
    assert!(source.contains("and not .removed"));
    assert_eq!(source.matches("[h] for h in range(hour(now) - 24, hour(now) + 1)").count(), 1);
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.clone())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    assert!(bound.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.notes);
    assert!(bound.schema.schedules.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(bound.schema.operations["trending"].runs.as_deref(), Some("server"));
    let forged = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("{source}\nmutation forge():\n  update posts['p'] {{ likeCount: 999 }}\n"))]);
    assert!(forged.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN"), "{:?}", forged.diagnostics);
    let public = source.replace("  insert <- .authorId = viewer", "  insert <- .authorId = viewer\n  sync public")
        .replace("  delete <- .principal = viewer", "  delete <- .principal = viewer\n  sync public");
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), public)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    assert_eq!(bound.notes.iter().map(|d| d.code.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["N_MAINTAINED_COLUMN_STREAM"]);
    assert_eq!(bound.schema.operations["trending"].runs.as_deref(), Some("device"));
    assert_eq!(bound.schema.operations["setLike"].predicts.as_deref(), Some("eligible"));
    for name in ["posts", "likes"] { assert!(bound.schema.tables[name].sync.as_ref().unwrap().horizon.is_none()); }
}

#[test]
fn retired_w15c_value_index_refusals_point_at_the_column() {
    for key in ["author, value, at, id", "author, value, at", "value, at, author", "at, value, author, id", "value, at, id", "author, value", "value, id, id", "author, author, value, id"] {
        let bound = compile(&format!("maintain totals: count posts by author, at\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n  by byCount: {key}\n"));
        let d=bound.diagnostics.iter().find(|d|d.code == "E_QUARRY_MAINTAIN").unwrap_or_else(||panic!("{key}: {:?}",bound.diagnostics));
        assert_eq!(d.message,"a maintain value index is a prefix of group fields, then value, id; fields cannot repeat");
        assert!(d.rewrite.contains("likeCount: count likes on posts"));
        assert!(d.rewrite.contains("posts last 20 by .likeCount, .at after c"));
    }
}

#[test]
fn w15c_old_value_indexes_and_expiry_refusal_are_unchanged() {
    for key in ["value, id", "author, value, id", "author, at, value, id"] {
        let b = compile(&format!("maintain totals: count posts by author, at\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n  by byCount: {key}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty() && b.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert_eq!(b.schema.tables["totals"].indexes["byCount"].columns(), key.split(", ").collect::<Vec<_>>());
    }
    expiring_rank_rewrite_routes_by_window_intent();
    let source = SOURCE.replace("  at: time", "  until: time\n  by byUntil: until\n  expire at .until\n  at: time");
    for key in ["author, value, at, id", "author, value, at"] {
        let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("expiry.q".into(), format!("{source}maintain totals: count posts by author, at\n  read <- allow\n  by byCount: {key}\n"))]);
        assert_eq!(b.diagnostics[0].code, "E_QUARRY_MAINTAIN_EXPIRE");
        assert_eq!(b.diagnostics[0].message, "maintain totals cannot order by value because source posts expires: stored order is stale before the physical sweep, so taking stored top-N can miss a live winner; a count upper-bound threshold scan is not built");
    }
}

#[test]
fn cards_pin_retirement_and_newest_first_column_recipe() {
    let maintains = snapback4_lang::guide::card("maintains").unwrap().split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    assert!(maintains.contains("A maintained view has rows `{ id, <group columns>, value }`."));
    let feeds = include_str!(concat!(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"), "/../snapback4-lang/tests/fixtures/legacy-feed-recipes.md"));
    let source = snapback4_lang::guide::quarry(feeds).into_iter().find(|s| s.contains("likeCount: count likes by postId")).unwrap();
    assert!(source.contains("by byHourLikes: hour(at), likeCount, at, id\n"));
    assert!(feeds.split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ").contains("Equal scores put the newest post first"));
    let readme = include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md").split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    assert!(readme.contains("`guide maintains`") && readme.contains("`guide feeds`"), "the orientation reaches both complete references");
    // The complete ordering law above and the recipe's key now live on these linked cards.
    let reference = format!("{maintains} {}", feeds.split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" "));
    assert!(reference.contains("by byHourLikes: hour(at), likeCount, at, id") && reference.contains("Equal scores put the newest post first"));
    assert!(!reference.contains("ties use maintained id") && !readme.contains("ties use maintained id"));
    let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty() && b.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn recorded_attribution_admission_and_nullable_keys() {
    let source = include_str!("fixtures/recorded-attribution.q");
    let compile = |source: &str| snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    let good = compile(source);
    assert!(good.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", good.diagnostics);
    let likes = &good.schema.tables["likes"];
    assert_eq!(likes.recorded.len(), 3);
    assert!(likes.columns["postId.at"].is_optional());
    assert!(good.schema.tables["trend"].columns["hour"].is_optional());
    assert!(good.schema.operations["like"].sites.iter().any(|s| s.table == "posts" && s.kind == "recorded attribution"));
    let types = snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&good);
    let likes_type = types.split("  likes: {").nth(1).unwrap().split("  };").next().unwrap();
    assert!(!likes_type.contains("postId."), "{likes_type}");
    for bad in [
        source.replacen("read <- allow", "read <- .authorId = viewer", 1),
        source.replace("  immutable postId\n", ""),
        source.replace("immutable authorId, parentId, at", "immutable parentId, at"),
        source.replace("by postId.authorId", "by postId.parentId.authorId"),
        source.replace("by postId.authorId", "by postId.unknown"),
        source.replace("where true by", "where postId.at > 0 by"),
    ] {
        let bound = compile(&bad);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_MAINTAIN" && d.guide == "maintains" && !d.rewrite.is_empty()), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    }
}

    13	    pub(super) fn as_viewer(&mut self, principal: &A, body: &[S], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
    14	        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Job || self.in_prelude || self.loops + self.caller_loops == 0 || self.viewer_depth.is_some() {
    15	            return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_CONTEXT", "as belongs inside a job sweep and cannot nest", "put as recipient.principal: inside for recipient in recipients:"));

    20	        }

    22	        self.loops = 0;
