    24	        self.clear_authority_refinements();
    25	        self.viewer_depth = Some(self.depth);
    26	        let mut code = setup::Body::default();
    27	        let result = self.block(body, &mut code, false);
    28	        self.clear_authority_refinements();
    29	        self.viewer_depth = None;
    30	        self.loops = loops;
    31	        self.caller_loops -= loops;
    32	        result?;

    66	            let module = std::path::Path::new(&doc.file).file_stem()?.to_str()?;
    67	            doc.programs.iter().find(|decl| super::super::operation_name(module, &decl.name) == name || module == caller_module && decl.name == name).map(|decl| (module.to_owned(), decl.clone()))

    72	            }
    73	            let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();

# Totals: count and sum in queries, mutations, jobs and views

Count rows or add up a column with `count` and `sum` inside a query, mutation or job. A view keeps a total per group current on every write, so reading it costs one row.

```quarry
table orders:
  owner: principal
  status: text <=10
  cents: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
view spentByOwner: sum orders of .cents by .owner
  online only
view spentPerDay: sum orders of .cents by .owner, day(.at)
  online only

query myTotals(since: time):
  open = count(orders where .owner = viewer and .status = 'open')
  recent = sum(orders where .owner = viewer and .at >= since, .cents)
  return { open, recent, lifetime: spentByOwner[viewer], today: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now)], yesterday: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now) - 1] }
```

There are two spellings, and they do not mix:

- **Inside an operation** (query, mutation, job): `count(t where …)` and
  `sum(t where …, .col)`. The parentheses are required; the column is the
  second argument, written `.col`. `min` and `max` take the same arguments.
- **In a view** (declared at top level): `view name: count t by .key` or
  `view name: sum t of .col by .key`, optionally `where …` before `by`
  (`sum t of .col where … by .key`, in that order). Read
  it by its group key, `spentByOwner[viewer]`; a group with no rows reads `0`.
  Omit `by` for one global scalar, `view orderCount: count orders`, and read it
  as `orderCount`. Its authorized empty value is `0` even though zero has no
  physical aggregate row.
  A view over an `online only` table is read online; its own `online only`
  line is optional.

## In a job

A job's totals are ordinary domain reads inside its sweep; write them where a
screen can read them:

```quarry
table readings:
  owner: principal
  value: int
  at: time default now
  immutable owner, at
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table sensors:
  owner: principal
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only
table dailyStats:
  owner: principal
  day: time
  n: int
  total: int
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- deny
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny
  online only

job dailyTotals(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):
  for s in sensors by .id:
    n = count(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd)
    total = sum(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd, .value)
    insert dailyStats { owner: s.owner, day: periodStart, n, total }
schedule nightly every 1d at 00:05: dailyTotals
```

`periodStart` and `periodEnd` are the schedule's period, so each run totals
one day. When the job only needs a number a screen shows, a view is usually
simpler than a job: `view perDay: sum readings of .value by .owner, day(.at)`
read as `perDay[viewer, day(now)]`.

## Rows the caller partly reads

An inline total counts only what its caller may read, so `check` refuses one
whose read rule its pins do not decide (`E_QUARRY_BOUND`, or
`E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT` in a mutation): it would be a different number for
each caller, and a capacity check would miss hidden rows. Three exact forms:
pin the deciding column (`.owner = viewer`: the caller's own total); or keep
the total in a view whose read rule names who sees it (the view discloses the
total, never the rows); or, in a mutation, `require` below a view's count.

```quarry
table staff:
  principal: principal
  role: text <=20
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  online only
table requests:
  owner: principal
  dept: text <=20
  amount: money USD
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
view requestsTotal: sum requests of .amount by .dept
  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
view requestsByOwner: sum requests of .amount by .owner, .dept
  read <- .owner = viewer

query deptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsTotal[dept] ! else NOT_ALLOWED
query myDeptTotal(dept: text <=20):
  return requestsByOwner[viewer, dept]
```

Finance reads every department's total; each requester reads their own.
`E_QUARRY_BOUND`'s rewrite prints this view for your table, column and group.
A keyed private view stays nullable until its own read rule is proved. For
example, after `require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
else NOT_FINANCE`, `requestsTotal[dept] + 1` is well typed and a readable
missing group contributes zero. A hidden stored group and a hidden miss both
remain null. A following write or viewer change invalidates the proof before
another keyed read.
A view may omit `by` for a whole-table scalar: `view requestsCount: count
requests`, read as bare `requestsCount`. The scalar still obeys its view read
rule: an authorized absence is zero, while a hidden stored or absent value is
null. Keep a real group key when callers need independent totals.
A guard on the read rule's own grant admits an inline total, as it admits a
staff page (`guide indexes`): after `require exists staff[principal = viewer,
role = 'finance'] else …` (or inside `if` on it), `sum(requests, .amount)` is
every row's total, because every caller who reaches it reads every row. A guard
on any other role does not; use the view.

For extrema over a domain, first establish that exact domain is non-empty:
`n = count(orders where .owner = viewer)` followed by `require n > 0 else
EMPTY`. From there, `min` and `max` of a required column on the identical
domain are required values; `sum` already has a zero identity. Optional projected columns remain optional. A
write, local rebinding, viewer/authority scope change, or different key/filter
invalidates the proof.

## Choosing

- A total a screen shows on every visit: a view. It is maintained on each
  write and read by key, constant work regardless of history.
- A rolling window (last 7 days): bucket the view by `day(.at)` (or
  `hour(.at)`, `bucket(.at, 15m)`) and add the buckets you need with point
  reads, `perDay[viewer, day(now)] + perDay[viewer, day(now) - 1]`.
- A one-off or filtered total (since an argument, by status): `count(...)` or
  `sum(...)` in the operation. It streams the domain under the meter: each
  row is authorized, filtered, folded and released. Its work grows with the
  matching rows, and `check --cost` says so; bound the predicate by an indexed
  time or a group key.
- `count(page)` and `sum(page, .col)` over a page you already read count only
  that page, not the population.

## Details

A meter cut never yields a partial total: the read is unavailable (the client
shows `loading`), including before `??`. On a device, an inline total needs
the device to hold its whole domain; otherwise the observation stays
`loading`, never a partial total. Sums take `int`, `decimal` or `money` columns; a total
that overflows refuses the transaction. `guide maintains` has every view form
(filters, reference paths, time buckets, offsets and sync); `guide reads`
covers totals beside pages and `exists`; `guide jobs` the sweep a job's totals
live in.

Common misspellings and what `check` says:

- `sum(orders of .cents)` (the view spelling inside an operation):
  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape of`. Write `sum(orders where …, .cents)`.
- `count orders where .owner = viewer` without parentheses:
  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape where`. Write `count(orders where …)`.
- `sum(orders where …, cents)`: `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown name cents`. The
  column is `.cents`.

Reference-grouped views over `read through .parent` rows inherit the parent's
rule. Omit a read line that repeats `.parent.owner = viewer`; it is
`E_REDUNDANT_RULE`, and deleting the whole line preserves the inherited audience.
Maintained sums spell the value separately: `view total: sum expenses of .amount by .household`,
not `sum expenses.amount by .household`.
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Types

Types bound columns, arguments, text, lists and uploads.

```quarry
table profiles:
  owner: principal
  name: text 1..80
  bio: text ?
  createdAt: time default now
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
mutation createProfile(name: text 1..80):
  return insert profiles { owner: viewer, name }
```

Reference contexts project required rows’ `.id`. Media: decimal KB/MB/GB.

`?` makes columns/arguments nullable; omission is null. Table references do not
read targets: use `require exists t[id] else CODE` or
`r = t[id] ! else CODE`.
Defaults fill omissions; explicit wins. Formats: handle/url/email; identity may
be stricter than the email contract below. Money/decimal are
scaled integers; fractions refuse E_INPUT. `divide` names rounding and int/int
result scale. Unique-read/positive-count guards narrow; `sum` has zero. Writes,
rebinding or viewer changes invalidate.

## Limits

- Guard or assert nullable values before passing them to required parameters: E_QUARRY_TYPE.
- Values and arithmetic must fit the integer range: E_OVERFLOW.
- `/` and `%` take ints and floor: `-7 / 2` is -4, `-7 % 2` is 1. A divisor must be nonzero: E_DIV_ZERO.
- `divide(value, int, mode)` preserves kind; modes are `toward-zero`,
  `away-from-zero`, `floor`, `ceiling`, `half-away-from-zero`, `half-even`.
  Zero: E_DIV_ZERO; overflow: E_OVERFLOW.
- `divide(int, int, scale, mode)` returns `decimal scale` (literal 0–12) with
  checked wide scaling and an i53 result. Null is not zero.
- New required defaults must also fit the existing store: E_ADOPT.

## Details

Types describe the values a column or argument accepts.
They also set limits on text, lists and uploaded assets.

### Column types

```quarry
table everything:
  who: principal                      -- the authenticated subject; `viewer` is one
  tag: id                             -- an opaque identity
  parent: everything ?                -- a reference to a table's row: its id
  n: int                              -- an integer within ±(2^53 − 1); there are no floats
  at: time                            -- milliseconds since the epoch; `now` is one
  ok: bool
  price: money USD                    -- money CUR [scale]: integer minor units, scale 2 unless given
  ratio: decimal 4                    -- decimal S: an integer scaled by 10^S
  name: text 1..80                    -- text <=N, or text M..N
  description: text
  handle: text 3..20 format handle    -- format handle | url is checked on every write
  site: text <=200 format url ?
  kind: enum('a', 'b')                -- a text literal in this position is a variant
  meta: json <=4000 ?
  raw: bytes <=1024 ?
  photo: image <=10000000 once ?      -- an upload (guide assets); `once` serves one view per principal
  clip: video <=50000000 ?
  tags: [text <=20] <=8               -- [T] 1..N | [T] <=N (0..N); `?` on the whole list, never the element
  unique handle else HANDLE_TAKEN 'That handle is taken.'
  read <- .who = viewer
  insert <- .who = viewer
  update <- deny
  delete <- deny

fn label(kind: text): kind
query enumLabel(kind: enum('a', 'b')):
  return label(kind)

query typed(c: cursor ?, handle: text 3..20 format handle):   -- arguments take the same types, plus `cursor ?`
  return everything[handle = handle]
```

### Null guards and ids

Null guards narrow identical pure helper calls; rebinding invalidates proof. Bind impure calls once; both calls remain metered.

Mixed-table references need explicit generic ids: `[exposedId(x), exposedId(y)]`.
For nested `[].id` conflicts, widen each projected field with `exposedId(row.id)`.

```quarry
table a:
  public
table b:
  public
fn exposedId(value: id): value
query ids(x: a, y: b):
  return [exposedId(x), exposedId(y)]
query nestedIds(x: a, y: b):
  return [{ id: exposedId(x) }, { id: exposedId(y) }]
```

### Int ranges

`int 1..5`, `int 0..` and `int ..100` bound values; every write and call
checks them (`E_INPUT`).

### Text bounds

`text` defaults to `text <=10000` characters in columns, arguments and helper
parameters. Write `text <=N` or `text M..N` when the bound is a product rule;
an indexed column must also fit the key budget (`guide indexes`).

`format email` accepts one ASCII `@`. Its 1–64 character local part uses
letter/digit/`!#$%&'*+-/=?^_`{|}~` dot-separated atoms; its 1–253 character
domain uses 1–63 character letter/digit/hyphen labels. Dots cannot bound/repeat;
hyphens cannot bound labels. `alice@localhost` is valid. Case/Unicode are not
normalized; store a normalized lookup when needed.

## Engine-assigned per-parent sequences

`ordinal: sequence by conversationId` is a transaction-assigned `int` whose
parent is a required reference. E_SEQUENCE_PARENT names a missing, optional or
non-reference parent column. Ordinal and parent are immutable. Do not supply a
value or default. Only `sequence by` declares an ordinal; a table named
`sequence` remains a reference type.

```quarry
table conversations:
  public 'shared chat identities'
  online only

table messages:
  conversationId: conversations
  ordinal: sequence by conversationId
  public 'shared chat history'
  online only
```

Counters survive deletion/reopen; store reset clears them. Adding/rebinding a
populated sequence refuses E_ADOPT with an empty-table rewrite; empty tables may
adopt it. E_SEQUENCE_EXHAUSTED refuses a transaction that would exceed the
maximum int. Create a new parent for subsequent writes; no value is consumed.
The schema carries `sequence-by`; upgrade engines/devices that refuse
E_SCHEMA_CAPABILITY. `guide writes` has prediction, ordering and the full recipe.

## Tables

For complete index, rule and expiry recipes, open `guide indexes`,
`guide rules` or `guide expire` directly.

- Every table has an implicit unique `id`.

- **Keys and search:** `unique handle` or `unique postId, principal` enforces
  a product key. Add `else HANDLE_TAKEN 'That handle is taken.'` for a visible
  collision's code and message. Named keys resolve ambiguous positional types. Use
  `search body, caption` for word-prefix search. Predicates and `by .at`
//! Search card, compiled with every other keyword card by tests/guide.rs.
pub const CARD: &str = concat!(r##"# Search: word prefixes, on the server and on the device

**Start here:** Declare `search` on text or enum columns, then use `matching`, order and a cursor; the planner builds the word index. This directory query uses the profiles schema below; reset the cursor when the text changes and show incomplete results honestly.

```quarry
query people(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return profiles matching q after c first 20 by .handle
```

- `E_QUARRY_TYPE`: a search column is neither text nor enum; use one of those types before listing it in `search`.
- `N_SEARCH_UNSCOPED`: authorization varies across the scan; pin the intended room, or preserve the wider domain and show partial results.
- `E_CURSOR_INDEX`: an explicit index lacks a total order; use `by .column` so the planner supplies the page index.

## Reference

### Search one conversation, newest first, paged

Use the conversation's unique index order (or equivalently `by .ordinal`):

```quarry
table conversations:
  read <- exists members[.id, viewer]
  sync to members[.id]
table members:
  conversation: conversations
  principal: principal
  unique conversation, principal
  cap 50 by conversation
  read <- exists members[.conversation, viewer]
  sync to members[.conversation]
table messages:
  conversation: conversations
  ordinal: sequence by conversation
  body: text <=2000
  search body
  unique byConversationOrdinal: conversation, ordinal
  read <- exists members[.conversation, viewer]
  sync to members[.conversation] last 500 by byConversationOrdinal
query searchChat(conversation: conversations, q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
  return messages[conversation = conversation] matching q last 20 by byConversationOrdinal after c
```

Both forms plan `(conversation, words(body), ordinal, id)`; the unique index still serves history and receipts.
A named plain unique index supplies its order; named bracket keys must sit inside its leading prefix. Omitting keys orders by the whole index. Other plain indexes use column order.
Existing integer ordinals work too: do not migrate to `sequence by` just to search (`guide sequences`).
Pass `next` as `c`; reset it when the conversation or text changes. An empty needle browses in ordinal order.
Keep ordinals stable while paging; refresh to include new matches before the cursor. Point decorations preserve the bare page (below).
When server and offline device searches scan the same set of messages, their ordering and examined-row,
index-entry, step and output-byte counts match. A device searches acquired history: show
`complete: false` honestly, even for an empty result beyond its horizon; follow `next` only when present.

Plain `check` counts and summarizes search warnings: unscoped completeness (`N_SEARCH_UNSCOPED`), word expansion (`N_SEARCH_WORDS`), opening refusal (`N_SEARCH_OPEN`), and per-hit roster work (`N_SEARCH_SHAPE`). `--verbose` adds explanations and rewrites; `--cost` adds execution costs.

An empty needle, including whitespace or punctuation that folds to no terms, browses in the query's order under the same read rule. This directory uses one query, ordered by handle, with `next` to continue; reset the cursor when changing the search text. For a screen that wants nothing until typing, put `if q = '': return []` before the read.

`search body, caption` declares the searchable columns. Enum columns use the same spelling, for example `search category`; their stored variant text is
tokenized exactly like text (`'coffee-shop'` contributes `coffee` and `shop`).
The planner supplies the words index: one entry per distinct word of the named text or enum columns, in that position, folded the same way everywhere
(NFC, lowercase, a word boundary at every character that is not a letter
or digit). The columns before it are the search's scope; the columns
after it order the page and end in `id`, so a cursor continues it. A
words index serves `matching` and nothing else, and cannot be unique.

Indexed tokens and query terms are both truncated to 64 Unicode characters
after folding (at most 256 UTF-8 bytes). Longer terms therefore search with
that truncated prefix. The full composite word entry, including scope,
ordering columns and appended id, must fit the portable 2048-byte index key.

`matching q` folds the text the same way. Its first term selects every
word it is a prefix of ("cof": coffee, coffers, cofounder — one seek each,
bounded by the meter for a uniform scope; at most 32 words for an admitted
non-uniform scope, whose page remains `complete: false` even below that bound), and those words
merge as ordered lanes into one page — newest first with `last 20 by .at`. Every further term must be a prefix of some word of the row ("cof
car" finds "COFFEE for Carol"). Mid-word text does not match; an expired row is gone at once;
an edit re-tokenizes. A uniform scope (the rule decided by the room)
costs one verdict per scope; with a total unique `(chatKey, principal)` key
on t, `.principal = viewer or exists t[.chatKey, viewer]` is decided by the
chat alone because the row is its own witness, so a roster page pinned by
the chat is complete and no second table is needed. An unscoped search under a per-room rule
checks the rule per row and is never `complete` (`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` names
it), except for the inferred ordinary server audience described below;
use the grouped or newest form below, or read on the device.

The newest form below merges the viewer's rooms by `at` and `id`, with
one verdict per scope when its prefix decides the rule. Its uniform lane
source runs under the examined meter: `check --cost` prints `lanes ≤ meter`;
an explicit `first N` above 50 still emits N_WIDE_MERGE. Use the grouped form,
which pages memberships and includes each chat's hits, when opening all rooms
cannot fit.
A pinned conjunct such as `exists members[.roomId, viewer]` can deny a scope
before word discovery even when another conjunct needs each row. Admitted
non-uniform scopes keep per-row costs and remain incomplete; hidden rows
there still spend the query-wide examined/index-entry budget. The admitted non-uniform scope's 32-word
bound records a word cut without cutting later lanes; operator
results name it in `word_cut_at`, separately from a meter cut in `capped_at`.
Device reads and uniform server reads expose `cut: 'words'` or `cut: 'meter'`
beside `complete`; `meter` wins if both limits cut. Non-uniform server reads
report `cut: null`: their reason is withheld, with `complete: false` still truthful.
`null` means "not disclosed here", not "not cut". Render "Results may be incomplete"
for `complete: false` with `cut: null`, even when empty; never "no matches".
The precise reason remains in development-only `Read.meter.cut`; `serve` never
returns that meter. If the server cannot answer the query, declare `query … held:`
to answer from the device; the server refuses it with E_QUERY_HELD. Call a held
query through a local client over acquired partitions; it has no server fallback. Use the grouped
membership read for a server answer.
Uniform word expansion is bounded by the meter. A cut during discovery or
opening returns `complete: false, cut: 'meter'` with no continuation.
Heap exhaustion clears the page: `data: null`, `complete: false`,
`cut: 'meter'`, no `next`.
Merged pages return no `next` for a non-uniform answer, an opening cut or a
word-limit cut. A resumable read-meter cut while an owning uniform lazy merge drains established heads
can carry `next` after its last fully shaped emitted row (§5.6), subject to final-answer eligibility, with `complete: false, cut: 'meter'`.
Heap exhaustion clears the whole page even during the drain; step exhaustion aborts and output exhaustion can refuse after materialisation.
A terminal full page need not carry `next`.
   683	            }
   684	        }
   685	        // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — one verdict per matching scope, before word discovery and shared by its generated lanes.
   686	        let parent_pinned = table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| {
   687	            index.columns().iter().take(scope.len()).any(|f| f == &t.reference)
   688	        });
   689	        let mut admitted = !parent_pinned && (self.trust_synced_read(table) || self.context.is_system() || visibility.decided_true || table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable());
   690	        let mut prefix_expiry = super::expiry::Pending::default();
   691	        self.private_scan_authority(&scan.table, visibility.uniform, admitted)?;
   692	        self.reserve_delivered_scan(table, visibility, admitted, false)?;
   693	        if !admitted && !visibility.uniform { self.non_uniform = true; self.components.note(|frame| frame.non_uniform = true); }
   694	        if terms.is_empty() {
   695	            return Ok(None);
   696	        }
   697	        if !admitted {
   698	            // Uniform word scopes can borrow their rule; simplification/cloning
   699	            // is needed only by the existing partial-conjunction traversal.
   700	            let partial = if visibility.uniform { None } else { rules::prefix_decision(self.schema, scan) };
   701	            if let Some(rule) = if visibility.uniform { Some(&table.rules.read) } else { partial.as_ref() } {
   702	            if visibility.uniform { self.meter.heap(key_verdict_heap(index, &scope, &self.context.viewer, rule))?; }
   703	            let aborting = self.meter.cut == crate::meter::Cut::Abort;
   704	            if aborting && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)? { return Ok(None); }
   705	            let (allowed, pending) = self.prefix_verdict(&scan.table, table, index, &scope, rule, visibility.uniform)?;
   706	            if !allowed && !(self.device && parent_pinned) {
   707	                return Ok(None);
   708	            }
   709	            prefix_expiry = pending;
   710	            admitted = allowed && visibility.uniform;

//! `view` shares the aggregate AST; planned and feed views have distinct nodes.
use super::*;

impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn parse_view(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {
        if matches!(self.peek_at(3).value.as_str(), "count" | "sum") {
            let maintain = self.parse_maintain()?;
            self.document.maintains.push(maintain);
            return Ok(());
        }
        let site = self.take("view")?.site;
        let name = self.binding_name()?;
        self.take(":")?;
        if !self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) {
            let source = self.word()?.value;
            self.take("where")?;
            let predicate = self.filtered_predicate(&source, &["<newline>"], false)?;
            self.newline()?;
            self.document.feed_views.push(FeedView { name, source, predicate, site });
            return Ok(());
        }
        self.newline()?;
        self.take_kind(Kind::Indent)?;
        let mut branches = Vec::new();
        while !self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) && !self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
            self.skip_newlines();
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) { break; }
            let source = self.word()?;
            let predicate = if self.maybe("where") {
                let predicate = self.filtered_predicate(&source.value, &["{"], false)?;
                Some(predicate)
            } else { None };
            self.take("{")?;
            let fields = self.with_focal(&source.value, |parser| parser.parse_shape_fields())?;
            self.take("}")?;
            self.newline()?;
            branches.push(ViewBranch { source: source.value, predicate, fields, site: source.site });
        }
        self.take_kind(Kind::Dedent)?;
        self.document.views.push(ViewDecl { name, branches, site });
        Ok(())
    }
}

pub(super) fn duration_unit(unit: &str) -> Option<i64> {
    Some(match unit { "ms" => 1, "s" => 1_000, "m" => 60_000, "h" => 3_600_000, "d" => 86_400_000, _ => return None })
}

pub(super) fn unfocal_key(key: &mut Expr) {
    match key {
        Expr::Member { base, name, site } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) => {
            *key = Expr::Name { name: name.clone(), site: site.clone() };
        }
        Expr::Member { base, .. } => unfocal_key(base),
        _ => {}
    }
}
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState};
use snapback4_lang::{compile, types::Ty};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

fn checked(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound {
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    bound
}

fn engine(source: &str) -> Engine {
    let bound = checked(source);
    Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap()
}

#[test]
fn global_count_is_zero_then_tracks_inserts_and_deletes() {
    let source = r#"
table entries:
  public
  insert <- allow
  delete <- allow
  online only
table moderators:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  read <- .principal = viewer
  online only
view total: count entries
  read <- allow
  online only
view privateTotal: count entries
  read <- exists moderators[viewer]
  online only
mutation add(id: id):
  return insert entries { id }
mutation remove(id: entries):
  delete entries[id]
  return null
query totalEntries():
  return total
query privateEntries():
  return privateTotal
"#;
    let bound = checked(source);
    let generated = snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(&bound);
    let native = snapback4_lang::emit::rust::rust(&bound);
    assert!(!generated.contains("$global") && !native.contains("$global"),
        "the physical discriminator leaked into generated application types");
    for expression in ["total first 1 by .id", "total[true]"] {
        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery bad(c: cursor ?):\n  return {expression}\n"))]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
            && diagnostic.message.contains("global maintained scalar")), "{expression}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    }
    for domain in ["total", "total first 1 by .id"] {
        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery badLoop():\n  for row in {domain}:\n    return row\n  return null\n"))]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
            && diagnostic.message.contains("global maintained scalar")), "{domain}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    }
    for expression in ["count(total)", "exists total"] {
        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery badAggregate():\n  return {expression}\n"))]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"),
            "{expression}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    }
    for expression in ["min((total where true by byId), .value + 0)", "max((total where true by byId), .value + 0)"] {
        let bound = compile(&[("bad.q".into(), format!("{source}\nquery badExtremum():\n  return {expression}\n"))]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_TYPE"
            && diagnostic.message.contains("global maintained scalar")), "{expression}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    }
    let mut engine = engine(source);
    let query = |engine: &mut Engine| engine.query("totalEntries", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
    let private = |engine: &mut Engine, viewer| engine.query("privateEntries", Context::new(viewer, BTreeMap::new())).unwrap().data;
    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Null, "an unauthorized empty global stays hidden");
    engine.seed([("moderators".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
        ("id", Value::Id("mod".into())), ("principal", Value::Id("alice".into())),
    ]))]).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(0), "authorized absence is the count identity");
    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden absence is not zero");
    for id in ["a", "b"] {
        let outcome = engine.mutate("add", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id(id.into()))]))).unwrap();
        assert!(matches!(outcome.state, WriteState::Sent { .. }), "{outcome:?}");
    }
    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(2));
    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "alice"), Value::Int(2));
    assert_eq!(private(&mut engine, "bob"), Value::Null, "a hidden stored scalar remains null");
    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("a".into()))]))).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(1));
    engine.mutate("remove", None, Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("id".into(), Value::Id("b".into()))]))).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(query(&mut engine), Value::Int(0));
    assert!(engine.store().rows("total").unwrap().is_empty(), "zero deletes the physical aggregate row");
    assert!(engine.store().rows("privateTotal").unwrap().is_empty(), "private zero also has no physical row");
}

   172	fn view_join(a: &Ty, b: &Ty) -> Option<Ty> {
   173	    let optional = a.is_optional() || b.is_optional();
   174	    let a = a.required();
   175	    let b = b.required();
   176	    let joined = if matches!(a, Ty::Id | Ty::Ref(_)) && matches!(b, Ty::Id | Ty::Ref(_)) { Ty::Id }
   177	        else if a == Ty::Null { b }
   178	        else if b == Ty::Null { a }
   179	        else if a.comparable(&b) { a.join(&b).ok()? }
   180	        else { return None; };
   181	    Some(if optional { joined.optional() } else { joined })
   182	}
   183	
   184	fn cursor_kind(ty: &Ty) -> Option<snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType> {
   185	    use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType as C;
   186	    Some(match ty {
   187	        Ty::Id | Ty::Ref(_) => C::Id, Ty::Principal => C::Principal,
   188	        Ty::Int => C::Int, Ty::Bool => C::Bool, Ty::Time => C::Time,
   189	        Ty::Text(max) => C::Text { max: *max }, Ty::Bytes => C::Bytes { max: u32::MAX },
   190	        Ty::Enum(variants) => C::Enum { variants: variants.clone() },
   191	        Ty::Decimal(scale) => C::Decimal { scale: *scale },
   192	        Ty::Money(currency, scale) => C::Money { currency: currency.clone(), scale: *scale },
   193	        Ty::Optional(inner) => C::Optional(Box::new(cursor_kind(inner)?)),
   194	        _ => return None,
   195	    })
   196	}
   197	
   198	fn projection_source(lane: &ast::MergeLane, name: &str) -> A {
   199	    lane.project.as_ref().and_then(|fields| fields.iter().find_map(|field| match field {
   200	        ShapeField::Field { name: field, value, .. } if field == name => Some(value.clone()),
   201	        _ => None,
   202	    })).unwrap_or_else(|| A::Member { base: Box::new(A::Focal { site: lane.site.clone() }), name: name.into(), site: lane.site.clone() })
   203	}

    48	fn read_view(view: &ViewDecl, read: &ReadExpr) -> Fallible<MergeExpr> {

    56	        let mut projection = BTreeMap::from([("id".to_owned(), focal("id", &branch.site))]);
    57	        for field in &branch.fields {
    58	            match field {

    60	                ShapeField::Field { name, value, .. } if name != "id" => { projection.insert(name.clone(), value.clone()); }
    61	                _ => return Err(type_error(&branch.site, "view branches list named fields and carry their source id implicitly", "write { post: .id, actor: .authorId, at }; omit an explicit id field").guide("reads")),

    92	            order_keys: source_order, matching: read.matching.clone(), range: read.range.clone(), site: read.site.clone() };

    96	        }

codex
A second gap remains: a planned view can use the global total as a row source and call `scan_at` directly, bypassing the scalar checks. The email validation, strict enum decoding, enum search and integer division paths look consistent with the stated contract; the two remaining concerns are in narrowing and global scalar boundaries.

//! E_FIELD_AUTHORITY at `check` for a scan the server always refuses: a page
//! over a table with a private field (`written by .owner`, not `readable by
//! audience`) whose read rule the scan does not decide. The engine refuses it
//! before walking a row (`private_scan_authority`): a bounded walk over rows
//! the caller may not read would show their population through its meter and
//! its cut, and the private field makes those rows the protected kind. The
//! test is the engine's own, made statically: the scan's visibility is not
//! uniform, not decided true by its pins, and the table is not readable by
//! everyone. Only the system (jobs) and a device trusting its synced partition
//! pass it, so queries and mutations that are not held are checked; a role
//! guard (`if`/`require` on a grant) does not change it.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#17.3 — wave 7 lane 098: `check` was green and `test` refused at run time.
use super::{catalog::Catalog, program::BoundProgram};
use crate::diag::Diagnostic;
use snapback4_core::{ir::{IndexKeyExpr, ProgramKind}, schema::Schema};

pub(super) fn check(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, programs: &[BoundProgram], diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    for bound in programs {
        if !matches!(bound.program.kind, ProgramKind::Query | ProgramKind::Mutation) { continue; }
        if schema.operations.get(&bound.program.name).is_some_and(|fact| fact.held) { continue; }
        let mut seen = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        for scan in snapback4_core::ir::scans(&bound.program) {
            // A word search decides its own lanes' visibility (the matching plan).
            if scan.prefix.iter().any(|key| matches!(key, IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_))) { continue; }
            let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { continue };
            if schema.maintain_for(&scan.table).is_some() || table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable() { continue; }
            let private: Vec<(&String, &String)> = table.written_by.iter().filter(|(_, authority)| !authority.audience).map(|(field, authority)| (field, &authority.owner)).collect();
            if private.is_empty() { continue; }
            let visibility = snapback4_core::engine::rules::scan_visibility(schema, scan);
            if visibility.uniform || visibility.decided_true { continue; }
            let Some((site, text)) = bound.sources.get(&scan.site) else { continue };
            if !seen.insert(site.to_string()) { continue; }
            let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { continue };
            let pinned: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> = index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(component, key)| key.scalar().and(component.column()).cloned()).collect();
            let deciding: Vec<String> = snapback4_core::engine::rules::deciding_fields(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read).difference(&pinned).cloned().collect();
            let fields = private.iter().map(|(field, owner)| format!("{}.{field} (written by .{owner})", scan.table)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
            let (field, owner) = private[0];
            let pin = if deciding.is_empty() { "pin the read rule's columns".to_owned() } else {
                format!("pin {} with where equalities (`{} where {} …`), so every row the page walks is one the caller may read", deciding.iter().map(|c| format!(".{c}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "), scan.table, deciding.iter().map(|c| format!(".{c} = viewer")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and "))
            };
            let parent = singular(&scan.table);
            let guarded = snapback4_core::engine::rules::grant_dominated(schema, &bound.program, scan);
            let guard = if guarded { "; the role guard before it does not change this: the server refuses before it walks a row, for every caller" } else { "" };
            let _ = catalog;
            diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY",
                format!("{} {}: `{text}` scans {} rows the caller may not all read (the read rule `{}` is not decided by this scan's pins), and {fields} is private: the server refuses this read at run time with E_FIELD_AUTHORITY (\"scope this read to a uniformly readable audience before scanning rows with private fields\"), because a walk over hidden rows shows how many there are{guard}",
                    bound.program.kind.word(), bound.program.name, scan.table, table.rules.read.quarry()),
                format!("{pin}; or move {field} into its own table keyed by the {parent} (`{parent}: {}`, `{owner}: principal`, the {field} column, `unique {parent}`, `immutable {parent}, {owner}`, `read <- .{owner} = viewer`, `insert <- .{owner} = viewer`, `update <- .{owner} = viewer`) and read it by key, so this page scans no private field; or declare `{field} … written by .{owner} readable by audience` if every reader of the row may see it", scan.table)).guide("rules"));
        }
    }
}

/// `visits` → `visit`: a column name for the reference to the parent row.
fn singular(table: &str) -> String {
    let name = table.strip_suffix("ies").map(|stem| format!("{stem}y"))
        .or_else(|| table.strip_suffix("ses").map(|stem| format!("{stem}s")))
        .or_else(|| table.strip_suffix('s').filter(|stem| !stem.ends_with('s')).map(str::to_owned))
        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("{table}Row"));
    if name.is_empty() { format!("{table}Row") } else { name }
}

codex
NOT READY

- **P1 — A stale count can borrow another result’s capture marker.** At `5e280d9d39:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/aggregate_facts.rs:129–133`, lookup matches the domain without checking that the marker’s third component identifies the guarded local’s current definition. Extend the existing self-dependent counterexample with a fresh capture:

  ```quarry
  bucket = 1
  bucket = count(buckets[bucket] by byBucket)
  fresh = count(buckets[bucket] by byBucket)
  require bucket > 0 else EMPTY
  return min(buckets[bucket] by byBucket, .score) + 1
  ```

  With two rows in bucket 1 and none in bucket 2, `bucket` becomes 2 and `fresh` becomes 0. The fresh marker is attached to `bucket` as a dependency, allowing the stale positive value to prove an empty domain nonempty. Require the marker to belong to the guarded result’s reaching definition.

- **P2 — Planned views bypass global scalar restrictions.** At `5e280d9d39:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/views.rs:48`, planned lanes call `scan_at` directly, bypassing the global rejection in `bind/program.rs:356–360`. Consequently:

  ```quarry
  view exposed:
    total { value }
  query leak():
    return exposed first 1 by .id
  ```

  can enumerate the physical row of `view total: count entries`. This reintroduces a global scalar as a page source and exposes physical occupancy. Reject normalized global sources during planned-view expansion or shared scan admission.

Both findings are traced from the committed source; no files were changed or tests executed.
